\mathcal{O}_{1}\sim\mathcal{O}_{4}
.

According to the matching rules, the relevant building blocks of the ChPT are

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{\mu}\\
\Sigma_{-}\\
\Sigma_{+}\\
e_{R}\\
e_{L}\\
B\end{array}\right)\,,

(213)

and the ChPT operators after matching are the ones composed of these fields invariant under both the Lorentz symmetry and the
SU(3)_{V}
symmetry with CP properties C-P+ and C-P- respectively. In particular, the lepton current should not change during the matching.

Let us consider
{\cal O}_{1}
as an example. It is C-P+, and the first two pure meson operators in the matching are

\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle[\Sigma_{-},u_{\mu}]u^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(214)

where at least 2
u_{\mu}
are needed to form the C-P+ operators. The nonvanishing operators with minimal derivatives are called the leading-order (LO) operators in the matching.
On the other hand, the LO meson-baryon operators are

\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\Sigma_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,.

(215)

According to the NDA formula in Eq. (
208
), the normalization tells

\left(\frac{\Lambda_{\chi}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}}\right)^{2}f^{2}\Lambda_{\chi}^{2}\left(\frac{\psi}{f\sqrt{\Lambda_{\chi}}}\right)^{4}\sim\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{2}}\psi^{4}\,.

(216)

With more
u_{\mu}
added, there are more operators such as

\langle\bar{B}[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{-}]B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}B[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{-}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}u^{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})-h.c.\dots

(217)

The normalization of them has one more factor
\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\chi}}
, thus are sub-leading.

Here we present the matching result of the operators
{\cal O}_{1}\sim{\cal O}_{4}
with at most 3
u_{\mu}
for the pure meson operators and at most 1
u_{\mu}
for the meson-baryon operators.

\displaystyle C-P+:\,{\cal O}_{1},{\cal O}_{2}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle[\Sigma_{-},u_{\mu}]u^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(218)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\Sigma_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{-}]B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}B[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{-}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}u^{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})-h.c.,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{+}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\Sigma_{+}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B[u_{\nu},\Sigma_{-}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}[u_{\nu},\Sigma_{-}]B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})-h.c.\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,,

\displaystyle C-P-:\,{\cal O}_{3},{\cal O}_{4}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{-}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle[\Sigma_{+},u_{\mu}]u^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\Sigma_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{+}]B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}B[u^{\mu},\Sigma_{+}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}u^{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})-h.c.\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{+}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\{\Sigma_{+},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\sigma^{\mu\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\Sigma_{-}Bu_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\{\Sigma_{-},u_{\nu}\}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Bu_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}B[u_{\nu},\Sigma_{+}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}[u_{\nu},\Sigma_{+}]B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})-h.c.\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,.

In addition, the EOM and the IBP for the operators will not affect the matching between the LEFT operators and the chiral Lagrangian. For example, one of the above operator

{\cal O}_{1}=(\bar{q}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\mu}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(219)

can translate to the operator

{\cal O}^{\prime}_{1}=(\bar{q}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\textbf{T}q)\partial^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(220)

through the EOM and IBP, while the
CP
properties and the spurion of the operators are the same, thus the particle spectrum in the hadronic level will be the same. When the lepton part is fixed, the two operators should have the same matching results.

Here we will show the equivalence of these two operators after the matching explicitly. According to the matching rules, the non-vanish leading chiral Lagrangian of the operator
{\cal O}_{1}
are

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle[\Sigma_{-},u_{\mu}]u^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(221)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\Sigma_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})\,.

In addition, the non-vanish LO chiral Lagrangian for
{\cal O}^{\prime}_{1}
seem to be different with
{\cal O}_{1}
due to the difference of the lepton part and become

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},u_{\mu}]\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

(222)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-}B\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}\Sigma_{-}B\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{+}B\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,.

According to Eq. (
168
), the IBP, and the EOM of the hadrons, we can obtain

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},u_{\mu}]\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle D^{\nu}\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\Sigma_{+}[D^{\nu}u_{\nu},u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

(223)

\displaystyle+\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle[\Sigma_{-},u_{\mu}]u^{\nu}u_{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\Sigma_{+}[u_{\nu},D^{\nu}u_{\mu}]\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

where the first equivalence uses the IBP, the second equivalence uses the EOM of
u_{\mu}
and Eq. (
168
). This means the pure meson operators matched from
{\cal O}_{1}
are equivalent to the ones matched from
{\cal O}^{\prime}_{1}
up to some operators of higher chiral dimension. Similarly, the meson-baryon part can also translate that

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle D^{\nu}(\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B)\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}BD^{\nu}\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

(224)

\displaystyle\sim\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\{\Sigma_{+},u^{\nu}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}B\Sigma_{-}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle D^{\nu}(\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}B)\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}BD^{\nu}\Sigma_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}B\{u^{\nu},\Sigma_{-}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle D^{\nu}(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle D^{\nu}(\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B)\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}BD^{\nu}\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})

\displaystyle\sim\langle\bar{B}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+\langle\bar{B}\sigma_{\mu\nu}B\{u^{\nu},\Sigma_{+}\}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})\,,

where the first equivalence uses the IBP, and the second equivalence uses the EOM of
B
and Eq. (
168
). The equivalence relations of other operators can also be obtained similarly. According to the heavy baryon projection Eq. (
175
)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}_{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})=\langle\bar{B}_{v}v_{\mu}B_{v}\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}e_{L})+...\,,

(225)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}B\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})=\langle\bar{B}_{v}v^{\mu}B_{v}\Sigma_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}e_{L})+...\,,

(226)

which means the meson-baryon part is also consistent at LO. The other operators in Eq. (
224
) with more
u_{\mu}
which can also be found in Eq. (
218
) are of higher chiral dimension. Thus the EOM and the IBP relations do not affect the matching result.
Although this example is about pure meson operators and meson-baryon operators, the same argument applies when matching to other operators such as the ones with more baryons.

4.2
Matching Example for Dim-6 and 8 LEFT Operators

In this subsection, we present 2 more examples and clarify more details of our matching procedure.

Firstly, we consider the dimension-8 four-fermion operators with two derivatives

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{5}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

(227)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{6}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{R})\,,

(228)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{7}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

(229)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{8}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{R})\,,

(230)

where the Lorentz indices are symmetric. Here
T
corresponds to the building block
Q_{\pm}
. The CP properties of the quark part of the operators
\mathcal{O}_{5}\,,\mathcal{O}_{6}
are
C
+
P
+, while the CP properties of the operators
\mathcal{O}_{7}\,,\mathcal{O}_{8}
are
C
-
P
-. Thus the relevant particle spectrum of the chiral Lagrangian is

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{\mu}\\
Q_{-}\\
Q_{+}\\
e_{R}\\
e_{L}\\
B\end{array}\right)\,,

(231)

and the Lorentz structures of the lepton part should be fixed. Thus the chiral Lagrangian for the CP eigenstates of the non-vanish leading term becomes

\displaystyle C+P+:

\displaystyle\langle Q_{+}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

(232)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,,

\displaystyle C-P-:

\displaystyle\langle Q_{-}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,,

thus the matching will be obtained as

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{5}\,,{\cal O}_{6}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\langle Q_{+}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

(233)

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,,

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{7}\,,{\cal O}_{8}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\langle Q_{-}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{-}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{+}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}BQ_{-}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\,,

\displaystyle(L\leftrightarrow R)\,,

and the higher order of the chiral Lagrangian can be also obtained similarly, which can be found in the tables in the next section. The Wilson coefficients of mass dimension according to the NDA formula in Eq. (
208
) are

\langle Q_{+}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{\chi}^{2}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{4}}\partial^{2}\psi^{2}\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\bar{B}\gamma^{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{D}}\negthickspace{}^{\nu}Q_{+}B\rangle(\bar{e}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\negthickspace\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftrightarrow}}{{\partial}}\negthickspace{}_{\nu}e_{L})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{\chi}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{2}}\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{2}}\partial\psi^{4}\,,

(234)

where the derivatives on the baryons are of no chiral dimension. The normalization of the meson-baryon operators here are suppressed by a factor
\frac{\Lambda_{\chi}}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{2}}
compared to the ones of the previous example in Eq. (
216
).

Secondly, we consider LEFT operators involving four quarks

\displaystyle C+P+:\mathcal{O}_{9}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)\,,

(235)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{10}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)\,,

(236)

\displaystyle C+P-:\mathcal{O}_{11}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)\,,

(237)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{12}=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)\,,

(238)

which may contain a lepton bilinear either free of electric charge or not. These operators are useful, for example, by adding a lepton bilinear
(\bar{e}e^{c})+h.c.
they describe the
0\nu\beta\beta
process. Regardless of the leptons, the corresponding chiral spurions are
\Sigma_{\pm}
and the chiral Lagrangian of the pure meson operators are

\displaystyle C+P+:\,

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{+}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\,,

(239)

\displaystyle\langle\{\Sigma_{+},\Sigma_{+}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\{\Sigma_{-},\Sigma_{-}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(240)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(241)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(242)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{-}\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(243)

\displaystyle C+P-:\,

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{-}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\,,

(244)

\displaystyle\langle\{\Sigma_{+},\Sigma_{-}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\{\Sigma_{-},\Sigma_{+}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(245)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(246)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(247)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{-}\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle\Sigma_{+}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle\Sigma_{-}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(248)

where only the LO and NLO operators are presented.
On the other hand, the LEFT operators can be combined as

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})\sim\mathcal{O}_{9}+\mathcal{O}_{10}-\mathcal{O}_{11}-\mathcal{O}_{12}\,,

(249)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})\sim\mathcal{O}_{9}+\mathcal{O}_{10}+\mathcal{O}_{11}+\mathcal{O}_{12}\,,

(250)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})\sim\mathcal{O}_{9}-\mathcal{O}_{10}-\mathcal{O}_{11}+\mathcal{O}_{12}\,,

(251)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})\sim\mathcal{O}_{9}-\mathcal{O}_{10}+\mathcal{O}_{11}-\mathcal{O}_{12}\,,

(252)

so that we recover the LEFT operators in terms of the symmetry
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
.
The LO ChPT operators after matching would be

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L}):\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle 2\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{-}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle-2\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{-}\rangle

(253)

\displaystyle-\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle-\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+...\,,

(254)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R}):\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle 2\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{-}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle+2\langle\Sigma_{+}\Sigma_{-}\rangle

(255)

\displaystyle+\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+...\,,

(256)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R}):\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle-\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle-\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+...\,,

(257)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{R}\textbf{T}q_{L}):\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle-\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle-\langle\Sigma_{-}\rangle\langle\Sigma_{+}\rangle+...\,,

(258)

which can be compared to the previous result about
0\nu\beta\beta

[
44
]
.

Similarly, we have another 4-quark structure composed of two vector (axial vector) bilinears,

\displaystyle C+P+:\,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{13}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{\mu}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma_{\mu}q)\,,

(259)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{14}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}q)\,,

(260)

\displaystyle C-P-:\,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{15}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma_{\mu}q)\,,

(261)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{16}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{\mu}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}\gamma_{\mu}q)\,.

(262)

The chiral spurions are
Q_{\pm}
and they would match to the chiral Lagrangian

\displaystyle C+P+\,:

\displaystyle\langle Q_{+}Q_{+}\rangle\,,

(263)

\displaystyle\langle\{Q_{+},Q_{+}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\{Q_{-},Q_{-}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,

(264)

\displaystyle\langle Q_{-}Q_{-}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle Q_{+}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle Q_{+}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle Q_{-}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle Q_{-}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,

(265)

\displaystyle C-P-\,:

\displaystyle\langle Q_{+}Q_{-}\rangle\,,

(266)

\displaystyle\langle\{Q_{+},Q_{-}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\langle\{Q_{-},Q_{+}\}u^{\mu}u_{\mu}\rangle\,,

(267)

\displaystyle\langle Q_{-}Q_{+}\rangle\langle u_{\mu}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle Q_{+}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle Q_{-}u^{\mu}\rangle\,,\langle Q_{-}u_{\mu}\rangle\langle Q_{+}u^{\mu}\rangle\,.

(268)

As argued before, we can combine the LEFT operators as

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})\sim\mathcal{O}_{13}+\mathcal{O}_{14}-\mathcal{O}_{15}-\mathcal{O}_{16}\,,

(269)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})\sim\mathcal{O}_{13}+\mathcal{O}_{14}+\mathcal{O}_{15}+\mathcal{O}_{16}\,,

(270)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})\sim\mathcal{O}_{13}-\mathcal{O}_{14}-\mathcal{O}_{15}+\mathcal{O}_{16}\,,

(271)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})\sim\mathcal{O}_{13}-\mathcal{O}_{14}+\mathcal{O}_{15}-\mathcal{O}_{16}\,,

(272)

and their LO ChPT operators after matching would be

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L}):

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle Q_{+}Q_{+}\rangle-\langle Q_{+}Q_{-}\rangle\,,

(273)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R}):

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\langle Q_{+}Q_{+}\rangle+\langle Q_{+}Q_{-}\rangle\,,

(274)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L})(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R}):

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt0\,,

(275)

\displaystyle(\bar{q}_{R}\gamma^{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{R})(\bar{q}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\textbf{T}q_{L}):

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt0\,,

(276)

which is consistent with Ref.
[
44
]
.
We have mentioned that a ChPT operator can be matched from more than one LEFT operator. The 4-quark operators here offer an appropriate example. If we consider the operators with 2 more derivatives

\displaystyle C+P+:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{9}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)D^{2}(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)\,,

(277)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{10}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)D^{2}(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)\,,

(278)

\displaystyle C+P-:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{11}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)D^{2}(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)\,,

(279)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{12}=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)D^{2}(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)\,,

(280)

the spurions and the CP properties are the same, thus the matching operators should not change. However, since of the two derivatives, these LEFT operators are of higher dimension, and thus their matching contributions to the ChPT operators are sub-dominant according to the NDA formula in Eq. (
208
).

4.3
The Matching Results Up to NLO

The spurion method is systematic and can be applied to any effective operators of the two EFTs.
In this section, we present the matching result of the LEFT operators listed in Sec.
2
. And the matching result can be classified by the number of spurions.
Because we only consider the LO and NLO operators of the pure meson and the meson-baryon sectors, some LEFT operators can match to nothing, whose nonvanish matching results are of higher order.
In particular, we match the dimension 9 LEFT operators about the
0\nu\beta\beta
to the ChPT ones, but we do not present the correspondences of the operators with scalar lepton bilinear such as
\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{1},\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{2},\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{7},\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{8}
, since they can be obtained from some ChPT operators by adding the bilinear, as shown in Tab.
37
.

5
Conclusion

The LEFT and the ChPT describe the quark interactions above and below the scale
\Lambda_{\chi}=1\text{ GeV}
respectively,
thus the matching between the operators of these two theories is important. Such matching has been considered for different procedures such as the kaon decay, the
0\nu\beta\beta
, and so on, of which the external source method is commonly used. As the precision measurements higher and higher, the higher-dimension effective operators of both the LEFT and the ChPT are demanded. However, the external source method and the old-fashioned spurion method are not convenient to do the matching as order of Lagrangian becomes higher and higher. On the one hand, the higher-dimension LEFT operators could contain complicated lepton and quark bilinears, which means the external sources introduced in Eq. (
2
) or the spurions under the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
symmetry in Eq. (
20
) are not adequate. Besides, the old-fashioned spurion method parametrizes the LEFT Lagrangian by the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
symmetry, and maps the quarks to the hadronic degrees of freedom one by one as shown in Eq. (
21
), which is complicated for the matching beyond the LO.

In this paper, we propose a systematic matching of any orders of LEFT operators to the ChPT operators using a single
SU(3)_{V}
spurions. We present an new formulation of the LEFT operators via only one spurion covariant under the
SU(3)_{V}
,

\mathbf{T}\rightarrow V\mathbf{T}V^{\dagger}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptV\in SU(3)_{V}\,.

(281)

Similarly, we construct the ChPT operators for the
SU(3)_{V}
symmetry using
\Sigma_{\pm}
and
Q_{\pm}
, which is related to the quark level spurion by the following dressing

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\pm}

\displaystyle=u^{\dagger}\textbf{T}u^{\dagger}\pm u\textbf{T}^{\dagger}u\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\pm}

\displaystyle=u^{\dagger}\textbf{T}u\pm u\textbf{T}^{\dagger}u^{\dagger}\,.

(282)

At the same time, the leptons and the photon are regarded as building blocks of the LEFT and the ChPT.

The matching between the LEFT and the ChPT utilizes the properties kept in both the quark level and the hadronic level including the spurion structures, the CP properties, and the non-quark fields. In the spurion method, any LEFT operators have ChPT counterparts in principle. In particular, the redundancies such as the EOM and the IBP do not affect the matching results. To assess the importance of the operators after the matching we use the NDA to normalize them.
Such an assessment is consistent with the power-counting of both the LEFT and the ChPT.
This spurion method is systematic and convenient when matching the high-dimension operators of both LEFT and ChPT.

Using the spurion matching method described above, we present the matching results of the LEFT operators of dimension 5, dimension 6, dimension 7, and part of dimension 8 and dimension 9. For the dimension 8 LEFT operators, we exclude the operators with two quark bilinears and one photon field, since the matching of them is similar with the pure quark operators.
For the dimension 9 LEFT operators, we only consider the ones with two quark bilinears and one lepton bilinear, which can be matched to the ChPT operators about the
0\nu\beta\beta
.
Because every LEFT operator can be matched to more than one ChPT operator, we only list the LO and NLO ones according to the NDA.

Finally, The matching procedure presented here for the LEFT and ChPT is quite general. Although we only list the matching operators in the pure meson sector and the meson-baryon sector, this spurion method is general and can be used in other sectors such as the 4-baryon sector. This matching procedure can also be applied to other theories, such as the dark matter direct detection, the axion searches, etc. These low energy processes probes high energy scale physics, which involve in several different scales.

Appendix A
Comparison to the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
Parameterization

With
SU(3)_{V}
spurions in Eq. (
2.1
), the relevant higher-dimension operators of the LEFT have been constructed in Sec.
2
. In this appendix, we present some examples to compare the operators here to the ones parameterized by the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
symmetry and illustrate that these two different symmetries are equivalent. We repeat here the equivalence means the independent coefficients are the same after the spurions get their VEVs.

Firstly, consider the dimension-5 operators, all the operators containing quarks are composed of a quark bilinear and a field-strength tensor of photon field. Due to charge conservation, there are 10 operators,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(5)}_{LEFT}\supset\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}}\sum_{a=1}^{5}c_{a}^{(5)}\mathcal{O}_{a}^{(5)}+h.c.\,,

(283)

where
c_{a}^{(5)}
are dimensionless coefficients and the five operators are

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{1}=F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{u}_{\mathbf{L}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}u_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(284)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{2}=F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{d}_{\mathbf{L}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}d_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(285)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{3}=F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{s}_{\mathbf{L}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}s_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(286)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{4}=F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{d}_{\mathbf{L}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}s_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(287)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{5}=F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{s}_{\mathbf{L}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}d_{\mathbf{R}})\,.

(288)

Firstly we organize the Lagrangian in terms of
q_{\mathbf{L}}
and
q_{\mathbf{R}}
as

\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}}F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{C}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q_{\mathbf{R}})+h.c.\,,

(289)

where
\mathbf{C}
is a matrix composed by the Wilson coefficients

\mathbf{C}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}c^{(5)}_{1}&0&0\\
0&c^{(5)}_{2}&c^{(5)}_{5}\\
0&c^{(5)}_{4}&c^{(5)}_{3}\end{array}\right)\,.

(290)

The Lagrangian is invariant under
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
if
\mathbf{C}
is covariant
\mathbf{C}\rightarrow L\mathbf{C}R^{\dagger}
.
Furthermore we combine
q_{\mathbf{L}}
and
q_{\mathbf{R}}
to form
q
utilizing parity symmetry,

q=(u,d,s)^{T}=\mathcal{R}\cdot(q_{\mathbf{L}}\oplus q_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(291)

the Lagrangian becomes

\mathcal{L}^{(5)}_{LEFT}\supset\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}}\left(F^{\mu\nu}\overline{q}\mathbf{C}_{1}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q+\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}\overline{q}\mathbf{C}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q\right)\,,

(292)

where the first operator is of positive parity and the second on is of negative parity. The coefficients are

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}\,2\text{Re}\mathbf{C}\,\mathcal{R}^{-1}=\frac{\text{Tr}\mathbf{C}_{1}}{3}\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{t}_{1}\,,

(293)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}\,2i\text{Im}\mathbf{C}\,\mathcal{R}^{-1}=\frac{\text{Tr}\mathbf{C}_{2}}{3}\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{t}_{2}\,,

(294)

where the matrices
\mathbf{t}_{1}
and
\mathbf{t}_{2}
can be prompted to spurion field
\mathbf{T}
.
Thus we obtain the final expression of the Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}^{(5)}_{LEFT}\supset\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}}\left[\frac{\text{Tr}\mathbf{C}_{1}}{3}F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{q}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q)+\frac{\text{Tr}\mathbf{C}_{2}}{3}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}(\overline{q}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q)+a_{1}F^{\mu\nu}(\overline{q}\mathbf{T}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q)+a_{2}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}(\overline{q}\mathbf{T}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q)\right]\,,

(295)

where
a_{1}\,,a_{2}
are two independent Wilson coefficients.
This expression is equivalent to Eq. (
283
) since the numbers of the independent operators are the same once the spurion
\mathbf{T}
gets VEV. The Lagrangian of
SU(3)_{\textbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\textbf{R}}
symmetry in Eq. (
289
) looks different though, the expression of
\mathbf{C}
in Eq. (
290
) implies that the number of independent operators is the same.

Such an argument illustrates the correspondence of the LEFT operators between the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
symmetry and the
SU(3)_{V}
symmetry,

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{L}},\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{R}})\oplus(\mathbf{3}_{R},\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{L})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbf{1}^{+}\oplus\mathbf{1}^{-}\oplus\mathbf{8}^{+}\oplus\mathbf{8}^{-}\,,

\displaystyle A_{i}^{j}(\bar{q}_{\mathbf{L}}^{i}\Gamma q_{\mathbf{R}j})+B_{i}^{j}(\bar{q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{i}\Gamma q_{\mathbf{L}j})

\displaystyle\sim c_{0}(\bar{q}\Gamma q)+c_{0}^{\prime}(\bar{q}\Gamma\gamma^{5}q)+c_{a}(\bar{q}\Gamma\mathbf{t}^{a}q)+c_{a}^{\prime}(\bar{q}\Gamma\gamma^{5}\mathbf{t}^{a}q)\,,

(296)

in which
A,B
are coefficients in the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
symmetry,
\Gamma
is a Dirac matrix, the superscripts on the right are the parity eigenvalues corresponding to the
SU(3)_{V}
irreducible representations and
a=1,2,...,8
. In addition, the translation of the coefficients for these two representations become

\displaystyle c_{0}\sim

\displaystyle A^{1}_{1}+A^{2}_{2}+A^{3}_{3}+B^{1}_{1}+B^{2}_{2}+B^{3}_{3}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{0}^{\prime}\sim A^{1}_{1}+A^{2}_{2}+A^{3}_{3}-B^{1}_{1}+B^{2}_{2}+B^{3}_{3}\,,

\displaystyle c_{1}\sim

\displaystyle A^{1}_{2}+B^{1}_{2}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{1}^{\prime}\sim A^{1}_{2}-B^{1}_{2}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{2}\sim A^{2}_{1}+B^{2}_{1}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{2}^{\prime}\sim A^{2}_{1}-B^{2}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle c_{3}\sim

\displaystyle A^{1}_{3}+B^{1}_{3}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{3}^{\prime}\sim A^{1}_{3}-B^{1}_{3}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{4}\sim A^{3}_{1}+B^{3}_{1}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{4}^{\prime}\sim A^{3}_{1}-B^{3}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle c_{5}\sim

\displaystyle A^{2}_{3}+B^{2}_{3}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{5}^{\prime}\sim A^{2}_{3}-B^{2}_{3}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{6}\sim A^{3}_{2}+B^{3}_{2}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{6}^{\prime}\sim A^{3}_{2}-B^{3}_{2}\,,

\displaystyle c_{7}\sim

\displaystyle A^{1}_{1}-A^{2}_{2}+B^{1}_{1}-B^{2}_{2}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{7}^{\prime}\sim A^{1}_{1}-A^{2}_{2}-B^{1}_{1}+B^{2}_{2}\,,

\displaystyle c_{8}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(A^{1}_{1}+A^{2}_{2}-2A^{3}_{3}+B^{1}_{1}+B^{2}_{2}-2B^{3}_{3})\,,\hskip 9.24994ptc_{8}^{\prime}\sim\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(A^{1}_{1}+A^{2}_{2}-2A^{3}_{3}-B^{1}_{1}-B^{2}_{2}+2B^{3}_{3})\,,

(297)

where
\sim
means the similar transformation in Eq. (
28
) is used.
Similarly, we can get the correspondences of the other representations, for example

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{L}},\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{L}})\oplus(\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{R}},\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{R}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbf{1}^{+}+\mathbf{1}^{-}+\mathbf{8}^{+}+\mathbf{8}^{-}:

\displaystyle(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}A\Gamma q_{\mathbf{L}})+(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{R}}B\Gamma q_{\mathbf{R}})

\displaystyle=c_{0}(\bar{q}\Gamma q)+c_{0}^{\prime}(\bar{q}\Gamma\gamma^{5}q)+c_{a}(\bar{q}\Gamma\mathbf{t}^{a}q)+c_{a}^{\prime}(\bar{q}\Gamma\gamma^{5}\mathbf{t}^{a}q)\,,

(298)

where the translation of the coefficients is the same as Eq. (
296
).

Considering the dimension-6 Lagrangian and beyond, the relevant operators can contain more than one quark bilinear. For the operators with single quark bilinears, the reformulations are similar, for example, the dimension-6 operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(6)}_{1}=(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{u}_{\mathbf{L}}u_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(299)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(6)}_{2}=(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{d}_{\mathbf{L}}d_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(300)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(6)}_{3}=(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{s}_{\mathbf{L}}s_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(301)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(6)}_{4}=(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{d}_{\mathbf{L}}s_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(302)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(6)}_{5}=(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{s}_{\mathbf{L}}d_{\mathbf{R}})\,,

(303)

can be reformulated as

\mathcal{L}^{(6)}_{LEFT}\supset\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\text{EW}}^{2}}\left[a_{1}(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{q}q)+b_{1}(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{q}\mathbf{T}q)+a_{2}(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{q}\gamma^{5}q)+b_{2}(\nu_{L}^{T}C\nu_{L})(\overline{q}\mathbf{T}\gamma^{5}q)\right]\,.

(304)

However, the operators with 2 quark bilinears such as the 4-quark operators of the dimension-6 Lagrangian can be complicated. For example, an operator is composed of two quark bilinears

A^{jl}_{ik}(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}^{i}\Gamma_{1}q_{\mathbf{R}j})(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}^{k}\Gamma_{2}q_{\mathbf{R}l})\oplus B^{jl}_{ik}(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{i}\Gamma_{1}q_{\mathbf{L}j})(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}^{k}\Gamma_{2}q_{\mathbf{R}l})\oplus C^{jl}_{ik}(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{L}}^{i}\Gamma_{1}q_{\mathbf{R}j})(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{k}\Gamma_{2}q_{\mathbf{L}l})\oplus D^{jl}_{ik}(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{i}\Gamma_{1}q_{\mathbf{L}j})(\overline{q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{k}\Gamma_{2}q_{\mathbf{L}l})\,,

(305)

the correspondence can be obtained by the tensor product that

\displaystyle\left[(\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{L}},\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{R}})\oplus(\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{L}},\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{R}})\right]\otimes\left[(\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{L}},\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{R}})\oplus(\mathbf{3}_{\mathbf{L}},\overline{\mathbf{3}}_{\mathbf{R}})\right]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left(\mathbf{1}^{+}+\mathbf{1}^{-}+\mathbf{8}^{+}+\mathbf{8}^{-}\right)\otimes\left(\mathbf{1}^{+}+\mathbf{1}^{-}+\mathbf{8}^{+}+\mathbf{8}^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=2\times\mathbf{1}^{+}+2\times\mathbf{1}^{-}+4\times\mathbf{8}^{+}+4\times\mathbf{8}^{-}+2\times(\mathbf{8}\times\mathbf{8})^{+}+2\times(\mathbf{8}\times\mathbf{8})^{-}\,,

(306)

where on the right the integers before the irreducible representations are their multiplicities.
The corresponding operators invariant under
SU(3)_{V}
are

\displaystyle C_{0}^{(1)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{0}^{(2)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}i\gamma^{5}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}i\gamma^{5}q)+C_{0}^{(3)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}i\gamma^{5}q)+C_{0}^{(4)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}i\gamma^{5}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}q)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(1)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(2)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(3)}(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(4)}(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{2}q)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(5)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(6)}(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(7)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{a}^{(8)}(\overline{q}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{2}q)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{ab}^{(1)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{b}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{ab}^{(2)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{b}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{ab}^{(3)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{b}\Gamma_{2}q)+C_{ab}^{(4)}(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{a}\Gamma_{1}q)(\overline{q}\mathbf{t}_{b}i\gamma^{5}\Gamma_{2}q)\,,

(307)

and the translation of the two representations’ coefficients become (repeated indices mean summations)

\displaystyle C_{0}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle A^{ik}_{ik}+B^{ik}_{ik}+C^{ik}_{ik}+D^{ik}_{ik}\,,

\displaystyle C_{0}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle A^{ik}_{ik}-B^{ik}_{ik}-C^{ik}_{ik}+D^{ik}_{ik}\,,

\displaystyle C_{0}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle A^{ik}_{ik}+B^{ik}_{ik}-C^{ik}_{ik}-D^{ik}_{ik}\,,

\displaystyle C_{0}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle A^{ik}_{ik}-B^{ik}_{ik}-C^{ik}_{ik}-D^{ik}_{ik}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{il}+B^{ik}_{il}+C^{ik}_{il}+D^{ik}_{il})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{il}-B^{ik}_{il}-C^{ik}_{il}+D^{ik}_{il})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jk}+B^{ik}_{jk}+C^{ik}_{jk}+D^{ik}_{jk})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jk}-B^{ik}_{jk}-C^{ik}_{jk}+D^{ik}_{jk})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(5)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{il}+B^{ik}_{il}-C^{ik}_{il}-D^{ik}_{il})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(6)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{il}-B^{ik}_{il}+C^{ik}_{il}-D^{ik}_{il})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(7)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jk}+B^{ik}_{jk}-C^{ik}_{jk}-D^{ik}_{jk})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{a}^{(8)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jk}-B^{ik}_{jk}+C^{ik}_{jk}-D^{ik}_{jk})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{ab}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jl}+B^{ik}_{jl}+C^{ik}_{jl}+D^{ik}_{jl})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{i}^{j}\mathbf{t}^{b}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{ab}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jl}-B^{ik}_{jl}-C^{ik}_{jl}+D^{ik}_{jl})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{i}^{j}\mathbf{t}^{b}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{ab}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jl}+B^{ik}_{jl}-C^{ik}_{jl}-D^{ik}_{jl})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{i}^{j}\mathbf{t}^{b}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

\displaystyle C_{ab}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle(A^{ik}_{jl}-B^{ik}_{jl}+C^{ik}_{jl}-D^{ik}_{jl})\mathbf{t}^{a}{}_{i}^{j}\mathbf{t}^{b}{}_{k}^{l}\,,

(308)

in which the index
\{i,j,k,l=1,2,3\}
and
\{a=1,2...,8\}
.

Up to dimension-8 and the dimension-9 operators with 1 lepton current, all the
SU(3)_{\mathbf{L}}\times SU(3)_{\mathbf{R}}
irreducible representations of the relevant operators are listed in Tab.
38
. All these operators can be reformulated similarly.

Lastly we consider a specific example of two quark bilinears and show that the independent operators with spurions are just the same as the LEFT operators after the VEVs has been taken. Consider the dimension-9 operators
\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{1}\,,\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{3}
and
\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{13}
, we present them here for convenience,

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{(9)}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}e^{c})+h.c.\,,

(309)

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{(9)}_{3}

\displaystyle=(\bar{q}\gamma^{5}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{q}\gamma^{5}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}e^{c})+h.c.\,,

(310)

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{(9)}_{13}

\displaystyle=(\bar{q}\gamma^{5}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{q}\textbf{T}q)(\bar{e}e^{c})+h.c.\,.

(311)

Because the lepton current is of electric charge
+2
, the VEV of
\mathbf{T}
is expanded by the matrices
\mathbf{t}^{1}\,,\mathbf{t}^{3}
according to Eq. (
95
). Considering the symmetry of the two quark bilinears, each operator above splits into several independent operators,

\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{1}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}d)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}s)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}s)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
\end{array}\right.\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{3}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
\end{array}\right.\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{O}^{(9)}_{13}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})+h.c.\\
\end{array}\right.\,.

(312)

These operators can be combine to be the conventional form composed of Weyl spinors,

\displaystyle(\overline{u}d)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}d_{L})(\overline{u}_{R}d_{L})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}d_{L})(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
\end{array}\right.\,,

\displaystyle(\overline{u}s)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}_{L}s_{R})(\overline{u}_{L}s_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}s_{L})(\overline{u}_{R}s_{L})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}s_{L})(\overline{u}_{L}s_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
\end{array}\right.\,,

\displaystyle(\overline{u}s)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}s)(\overline{u}d)(\overline{e}e^{c})\,,(\overline{u}\gamma^{5}d)(\overline{u}s)(\overline{e}e^{c})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\overline{u}_{L}s_{R})(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}s_{L})(\overline{u}_{R}d_{L})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{R}s_{L})(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
(\overline{u}_{L}s_{R})(\overline{u}_{L}d_{R})(\overline{e}^{c}e)\\
\end{array}\right.\,.

Thus we obtain the independent LEFT operators of this type, where the last four operators are about kaon decay and has been discussed in literature such as Ref.
[
53
]
.
This example offers one explicit evidence that the two different symmetries are equivalent.
